# civ64 port — status ledger

Single ledger of the x64 port (`claude/docs/x64-port-prompt.md`, section 11). Update it at every
gate, at session end and whenever a build or test result changes.

**Phase R: resource lookup (user requirements, 2026-10-10 about 16:40 UTC, verbatim):**

> x64 Application shall check following for the required resources in specified order;
> 1. ZIP files named CivWin.zip, Civ-Win.zip or Civ_Win.zip for Windows version of the game,
>    filename lookup shall be case insensitive.
> 2. ZIP files named CivDos.zip, Civ-Dos.zip or Civ_Dos.zip for DOS version of the game, filename
>    lookup shall be case insensitive.
> 3. Sub folder under x64 executable folder named CivWin for files of Windows version of the
>    game, folder lookup shall be case insensitive.
> 4. Sub folder under x64 executable folder named CivDos for files of DOS version of the game,
>    folder lookup shall be case insensitive.
> 5. If none of them found, then shall ask and browse Windows version, and DOS version of the game
>    seperately, and saves their user selected locations to remember later. Resources DOS version
>    of the game shall be optional, since it is a secondary mod to the Windows game.
> 6. preserve --data commandline argument

User decisions on questions asked at session start (2026-10-10 about 16:55 UTC):
* Concurrency: "Start after that run ends" — the base track's post-delivery fixes have stopped;
  the extension starts once the base session's `test.cmd asan` run (16:43..16:57) ended.
* ZIP writes: "Extract to a per-user folder" — first use extracts the ZIP to
  `%LOCALAPPDATA%\civ64\CivWin\`, which becomes the data directory; when the ZIP changes later,
  game files are refreshed and the user's saves are kept.
* Ordering: "After S2, before D0" — phase R runs after S2, so it is done in this session and the
  DOS asset loader (D2) can use the CivDos lookup instead of a required `--dos-data`.

**Extension blockers:** none.

**CY follow-ups (open; not done, each needs a decision; [ext-CY-record.md](ext-CY-record.md) "For the
records"):**
* A parallel option for `nodos.py check` (one process scanning 1,646 files, 1.47 GB): the whole floor of
  CheckedNoObs and Release (about 450..550 s) and a 1,000 s section of Checked; it touches a check's own
  code path, so CY left it.
* Compiling one variant's sources at the same time (`/FS` or a per-source `/Fd`): a compiler-option change,
  excluded by CY's brief; a single-variant build would go from about 280 s towards an estimated 60..80 s.
* A larger pool in `ext_p0.py` (8 processes, about 980 s, then `ext_mirror.py`: Checked's floor), a runner
  option of that script.
* Tuning the budget `CIV_TEST_BUDGET` (20); the sections' `est=` only set the first start order.
* Asan: prove `test.cmd asan parallel` against `serial` with `cy_equiv.py` at the final Asan pass, then make
  parallel its default.
* Per-process coverage files, so that `coverage.py`'s informational report no longer depends on concurrent
  appends (661 against 663 between runs).
* `coverage-civ_harness.txt` gets torn lines from parallel harness appends (owner: X9; [ext-FCE-record.md](ext-FCE-record.md)
  "Landing" 7, and D6f's landing): concurrent appends of the parallel harness runs to
  `build/x64/Checked/coverage-civ_harness.txt` tear lines (FCE's cycle -r2: 4 malformed keys, such as `11E0:01110:1767`
  and `368`), which `coverage.py` counts as functions, so its informational "other functions entered" moves from run to
  run (668 -> 666 at FCE's landing, 666 -> 664 at D6f's, 664 -> 663 at the D6 phase gate's, [ext-D6-acceptance.md](ext-D6-acceptance.md) "Known differences and open items"). For coverage.py or host_coverage; not changed by any of these units.

**UX1 open items (for their owners; not UX1's files; [ext-UX1-record.md](ext-UX1-record.md) deviation 3, UX1b's
deviation 4 and "For the records"):**
* `platform/w16/host.h` (owner of platform/w16): the comment of `host_set_cursor_pixels` still says "in logical
  pixels of the presented frame ... scales it as it scales the game's cursor"; since UX1 the pixels are the
  pointer's own and the back end draws them at `present_pointer_factors`.
* `tests/headless/harness.c` (owner of the harness): the host log names the command by a range ending at
  `PI_CMD_ZOOM_RESET`, so a scripted Alt+F4 in host form would log `cmd` as entry 0; add `PI_CMD_CLOSE` to the
  command names (no harness script sends F4; the harness has no host window and takes no exit).
* `present/present.h` (the BS landing's file): the comment of `present_pointer_factors` still says "so the DOS
  pointer has the Windows game cursor's pixel size"; since UX1b the pointer lies between its size before UX1 and
  the Windows cursor's ([ext-UX1-record.md](ext-UX1-record.md) "Deviations (UX1b)" 4).
* `platform/win32/host_win32.c` (the BS landing's file): the comment of `cursor_factors` still says "the Windows
  game cursor's pixel size, not the 320x200 frame's about twice larger factor" (UX1b deviation 4 likewise).

**Test open items (for their owners):**
* `w32_test_host_win32` flake (owner: X9): 2 of its 329 checks (`tests/win32/test_host_win32.c` lines 1206 and 1240:
  Ctrl+Backspace and the later refusals' beeps) fail when Shift or Ctrl is held on the machine's real keyboard during
  the run, because `host_win32.c`'s `key_down` reads Ctrl and Shift with `GetKeyState` while the test feeds window
  messages. Seen once in SL's landing cycle (`build/sl-2-test-checked.log`, TESTS_FAILED=1); the same binary passed
  three reruns and the rerun of the step (RUNNING.md "Tests"). Not fixed.
* `p49-findcity` (owner: the base track; an observation of unit MZS, not MZS's, [ext-MZS-record.md](ext-MZS-record.md)
  "For the records"): in the harness Escape does not close Find City (dialog 131, RunListBoxDialog 1098:1909); the
  scenario's comment says it does, but its events show the dialog open to the run's end. Answered by unit FCE
  (2026-10-17, [ext-FCE-record.md](ext-FCE-record.md) findings 1..6): the original under Windows 3.1 ignores Escape
  there too (IsDialogMessage turns it into IDCANCEL, which no game dialog has), so the run is faithful; the comment in
  `p49-findcity` ("Escape closes it") and `ext_zoom.py`'s MZS note are wrong about the Windows graphics (a comment fix
  for their owner). In the DOS graphics Escape closes Find City since FCE (FIDELITY F193).
* Escape in Windows Customize World (owner: X9; from unit FCE's audit): Escape in Windows Customize World (dialog 143)
  writes -1 to worldParams[-200] (12D8:3764, inside g_advanceSource) in the original too, and the port reproduces it
  faithfully. Unit FCE checked its effects (2026-10-17, [ext-FCE-record.md](ext-FCE-record.md) "Customize World's
  Escape"): the int16 at 12D8:3764 is g_advanceSource[2][36..37], inside the emulated DGROUP (no fault, HARNESS_OOB 0),
  and has no effect on the game (InitCivilization sets g_advanceSource to -1 for every civ after Customize World; a load
  replaces it); in the DOS graphics the code is not reached (D5e's I-custom takes Escape). Recorded as FIDELITY F195.
  Open for X9: confirm the write's target and its harmlessness in the final audit.
* `manual-checklist-ext.md` P47-2 (passed 2026-10-15; its rows are the orchestrator's): its text says "Enter and Escape
  still work and the dialog closes", but in the Windows graphics Escape never closed Find City (the original's
  behaviour, FCE finding 6), only Cancel or Enter did. A wording note from FCE's record, not a new check; the docs pass
  left the row unchanged.

Update 2026-10-16 11:34:48 UTC: unit **D5K** is done ([ext-D5K-record.md](ext-D5K-record.md) "Landing"; the user's D5-6
report, "User directions" 2026-10-16 about 10:10): the keyboard lost after the Windows-only Go Back. Cause: back on the
difficulty page MenuZ disables the focused Go Back (1220:07FC), EnableWindow leaves no focus window (Windows 3.1's rule)
and the keys reach the active window as system keys, which MenuZ ignores, in both graphics. D5K's fix is in the DOS
filter stage only (`present/dos/pdos_adapt.c/.h`): while the setup MenuZ waits without a focus, a DOS key reaches it in
its Windows mouse form (Return as a click on OK; Up or Down as the item's click and a press on OK released off it), so
the first DOS key gives the keyboard back (FIDELITY F183; DESIGN.md 12.8 "As implemented (D5K and D5K2; area C)"); the
core, the adapter rows and the Windows profile unchanged. The four staged files and the record were copied in after
every base was re-verified at 11:07:30 UTC (`build/d5k-prep/land.py`); the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 11:34:48 UTC

The clamp changes the offset, and the changed offset is then kept: after the view has been within about half a layer height of a pole (or the zoom-out happened there), the core's view, and the unit the core centred in it, stays displaced towards that pole for the rest of the session at -1, even far from the poles (a ratchet; vertical only, because `Xo` has no world clamp; +1 follows the unit by its own rule, 11.3, so it is unaffected). Proposed fix for session E3: keep the chosen place (the anchor's offset) apart from the clamped origin and clamp from the chosen place at each view change, or re-centre the layer on the view at each view change at -1; near the poles the clamp still keeps the unit off centre unless rows beyond the poles may show (a choice for the user). Session E3 notified by message; it named the fix unit **MZ** (staging in `build/mz-stage/`, ahead of D6e, reproduced first): at -1 every core centring (C or automatic) puts the core view's centre at the client's centre, the offset never ratchets, a wheel or menu zoom's anchor lasts until the next core view change; near a pole the unit may stay off centre (no rows beyond the poles, as in the game's own view); checks MZ-1.. with its user build, after UX4, D5P and a joint docs pass |

## Process notes

* Independence (2.3): while checking the toolchain, `scripts/Setup-Msvc.cmd` was read once
  (it only locates vcvars64.bat; no game facts). The port only executes it from
  `build.cmd`/`test.cmd`, as section 5.0 requires. No other file under the excluded trees was
  opened.
* `claude/tools/showfunc.py` is run for listing probes (allowed by 2.3); `claude/docs` and
  `claude/src` are the only sources of game facts.
* Verifier process deviation (first verification, 2026-10-08): one verifier subagent reported
  one read-only shell command combining `cd` and `grep`, against the session's no-`cd` working
  rule. It was a read; the deviation does not affect its findings. The re-verification briefs
  repeat the rule.
* Verifier process deviations (re-verification, 2026-10-08): the seg006_1028/seg062_11E8
  verifier reported one read-only command starting with `cd` (the rest used absolute paths), one
  inline `python -c "print(1)"` and temporary capture files in the session scratchpad (outside the
  repository). None of them wrote to the repository beyond its two report files; its findings are
  unaffected. The other re-verifiers reported no deviations.
* The re-verifier of the seven small units (finished 2026-10-08 20:29) reported one read-only
  grep/line-count command beginning with `cd` (absolute paths otherwise; nothing changed). It wrote
  only its seven report files; its findings are unaffected.

Phase 4 plan (2026-10-09 15:36, done). Game facts (survey of `claude/src/c`): the window procedures
handle none of WM_SYSCOMMAND, WM_SYSKEY*, WM_NC*, WM_INITMENU*, WM_MENUSELECT or WM_SETCURSOR, so
menus, scroll bars and the frame work through DefWindowProc. The map window "CIVILIZATION"
(0x02FF0000) has a caption, system menu, sizing frame, minimize/maximize boxes, both scroll bars
and the class menu. Leaf tasks, in order (weight of phase 4's 20 %):
1. P4-1 (2) Mouse routing: script mouse input at screen positions; the window from the position
   unless captured; DefWindowProc WM_NCHITTEST; client and non-client button messages;
   WM_SETCURSOR; activation on click; double-click for CS_DBLCLKS (metrics in DESIGN.md 10);
   `test_user` cases.
2. P4-2 (2) Non-client drawing: caption text, system-menu box, minimize/maximize buttons, menu bar
   (items, mnemonic underlines, grayed text) and scroll bars (arrows, thumb); FIDELITY F26.
3. P4-3 (3) Menu tracking: mouse (WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN on the bar) and keyboard (Alt, F10, Alt+letter)
   modal menu loop in DefWindowProc with WM_INITMENU, WM_INITMENUPOPUP, WM_MENUSELECT, popup
   drawing, then WM_COMMAND; grayed and disabled items give no command; Q5 decided.
4. P4-4 (1) Scroll-bar tracking (WM_HSCROLL/WM_VSCROLL codes, repeat on the virtual clock).
5. P4-5 (1) System commands: close (Alt+F4, system menu), minimize/maximize/restore, move and size
   by caption and border drags (Q12).
6. P4-6 (1) MessageBox as an emulated modal box (7 callers: start-up and error paths).
7. P4-7 (2) The rest of the controls and GDI: the EDIT messages the game sends, CreateDIBitmap
